### Gut and liver: the light-and-deuterium axis
- **id:** gut-liver-axis
- **pillar:** support
- **app:** hold
- **summary:** Kruse treats the gut as a light-and-deuterium organ flushed each morning by pancreatic bicarbonate, and the liver as the first organ to fail, linked to brain and pelvis by one fluid axis.
- **protocols:** none

In Kruse's model the liver is the first organ to fail and the gut is an organ run by light and deuterium, a heavy form of hydrogen. One shared fluid route links liver, pelvis, gut and brain, so he expects their diseases to travel together. The gut's job, as he sees it, is to flush deuterium out each morning, and most gut disease follows when that flush backs up.

#### Why the liver goes first

The liver carries the body's largest daily load of fluid, electrons and deuterium and serves as its deuterium sink, so Kruse says its mitochondria fail first. He reads high liver enzymes with a clean scan as deuterium overload, and points to antibodies against mitochondria in about 95% of primary biliary cholangitis. Fatty liver, to him, is a pressure-relief valve: when nerve noise breaks the gut barrier, the liver packs its cells with fat to shield them from singlet oxygen, a reactive form of oxygen.

The portal vein, liver and pelvic organs share one fluid conveyor that runs on up to the brain. That, he says, is why ovarian failure, sperm DNA damage, gut disease, brain fog, abnormal lipids and early artery disease cluster in the same people, rather than because of family history.

#### The morning bicarbonate flush

Morning sunlight's infrared, in Kruse's account, sets off the cortisol awakening response, the natural hormone rise after waking. That switches on the glucagon gene network on chromosome 2 and releases about 2 liters of bicarbonate fluid from the pancreas. This downward flush, guided by the vagus nerve, sweeps deuterium out through the gut, and a well-formed (Bristol type 4) stool shows it is working. Skipping food at the cortisol peak, he says, stalls it.

Diet can help or hinder the flush. He points to the Hunza people, whose roughly 100 grams a day of fiber from apricots and whole grains acts as a "deuterium sponge" in the gut, since plant cellulose is naturally low in deuterium; it shields the vagus and frees the bicarbonate loop to do its work. Plain baking soda, he adds, can stand in for missing pancreatic bicarbonate: it flushes deuterium and raises acetylcholine, the vagus nerve's chemical messenger, which tells the spleen and immune system the crisis is over and ends what he calls the cell danger response, the cell's defensive lockdown.

When the vagus fails in an ungrounded person under blue LEDs and nnEMF (wireless and wiring fields), the flush stalls and reverses. He calls this an "isotopic backwash": heavy water is pushed into the stomach lining, the valve at the bottom of the esophagus and the crypts of the colon. Reflux, hiatal hernia, colon polyps and colon cancer are, to him, one "hydraulic failure," with polyps a primitive low-voltage shelter that comes before cancer.

Kruse saw the same backup as a surgeon. After truncal vagotomy, the old ulcer operation that cuts the main vagus trunks, patients developed gallstones, dumping syndrome and gastroparesis (a stalled stomach), and his first rupture of a spleen without injury came two weeks after one. He reads all of these as gut organs filling with deuterium once the exhaust was cut.

#### The gut as a light organ

Gut bacteria, Kruse says, give off far more faint light (ultra-weak photon emission) than human cells, and that light organizes the gut's water. The gut lining sheds its cells every day or two, carrying deuterium away with them. The nerve route from the brainstem to the abdomen's nerve hubs (the celiac ganglion and the organ of Zuckerkandl) depends on melanin, the light-absorbing pigment, and blue light and nnEMF crash it.

A broken body clock shows up here first. Citing Li Cheng and colleagues (2026, *Microbiological Research*), Kruse says circadian disruption raises sebacic acid in the gut and shifts the microbiome away from Akkermansia, a mucus-protecting species; the mucus thins and immune B cells inflame the lining. The leaky barrier then speeds deuterium into the mitochondria, which he says is why shift workers have more leaky gut, inflammatory bowel disease and metabolic disease.

The microbiome then turns inflammatory, producing SIBO, Crohn's, colitis and leaky gut; deuterium favors the wrong bacteria, he says, and medicine then blames the bacteria. Crohn's lesions form where heavy water settles unevenly in the gut wall. Cutting out a Crohn's segment, he adds, only detunes the gut, which behaves like a tuned antenna. Intussusception, the bowel telescoping into itself, he calls a motor stall rather than a mechanical accident: deuterium clogs the energy motors of the gut's muscle and nerves, which lack insulating myelin and so are especially exposed.

#### Signs and gauges

When the abdominal melanin network fails, Kruse says, pigment gathers at boundaries as an emergency shield: melasma on the face and a darkened colon wall (melanosis coli) seen at colonoscopy. Polycythemia vera, a condition of thickened blood, traps heat and deuterium in small vessels like a stalled ocean current.

First-morning urine should be dark, he says, as proof of an overnight deuterium dump; clear urine means heavy isotopes were held back. He also reads tests as gauges of the interior. High homocysteine and TMAO (a by-product of gut bacteria) are "silt" markers showing the vagus and the bicarbonate flush aren't clearing deuterium, while white-matter changes on MRI and slow flow on thermography show body fluids thickened like "ketchup."

#### In practice

No separate protocol is linked here, but Kruse's advice follows from the model. Stand barefoot on wet sand, surf or dewy grass at dawn with sun on skin and eyes, and make the bedroom a "magnetic sanctuary" with all wireless off so the liver can clear deuterium in sleep. Eat a morning meal near the cortisol peak so the bicarbonate flush can run. Avoid ungrounded high-rise living, and avoid surgery for Crohn's where possible. Instead of waiting for structural disease, he tracks four markers: morning glucose, LDL, vitamin B12 and pigment changes such as melasma, and adds homocysteine, TMAO, a Bristol type 4 stool and dark first-morning urine as signs of whether the exhaust is clearing.

- **sources:** [384737](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/384737/), [384739](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/384739/), [380983](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/380983/), [384732](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/384732/), [374819](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/374819/), [374284](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/374284/), [375734](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/375734/), [375886](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/375886/), [382730](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/382730/), [382565](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/382565/), [372138](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/372138/), [372499](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/372499/)

### Graves', thyroid and the eyes
- **id:** graves-light-circuit
- **pillar:** support
- **app:** hold
- **summary:** Kruse traces Graves' disease, thyroid and adrenal flares and several eye diseases to a broken eye-to-brain light circuit plus night light and wireless.
- **protocols:** graves-and-eyes, brain-eye-ear-conditions

Kruse traces Graves' disease, thyroid flares and several eye diseases to a broken light circuit running from the eye to the brain's master clock, made worse by night light and wireless signals. Fix the eye's light and magnetic inputs, he argues, and the thyroid loses its upstream drive.

#### The broken circuit

Graves' disease, an overactive thyroid often with bulging eyes, is to Kruse a downstream symptom of a broken retina-to-hypothalamus pathway, the circuit that sets the body's clock and temperature. Mistimed blue light bleaches melanopsin, the retina's daylight sensor, so the master clock in the brain can't read the time. This is worse in an "unpinned" location, one where a person isn't grounded under a stable magnetic field.

The hypothalamus then loses its metabolic set point, and the thyroid overdrives to cover what it reads as an energy shortfall. Meanwhile nnEMF (wireless and wiring fields) opens calcium channels in the eye muscles, raising their metabolic rate and loading them with deuterium, a heavy form of hydrogen. A higher metabolic rate is exactly the wrong direction in Graves', he says.

The same failure reaches the adrenal glands. A third ventricle (the fluid chamber beside the hypothalamus) heavy with deuterium stretches the window in which thyroid-stimulating hormone (TSH) can do its repair work, and a stalled thyroid forces the adrenals to over-rev on adrenaline to keep the cell's energy motor spinning. That ends in the adrenal burnout of Addison's disease, which he says doctors treat as permanent.

#### Heme, histamine and lost tolerance

Heme, the iron-containing molecule in blood and many enzymes, is the body's magnetic antenna in Kruse's model, and immune tolerance depends on it. Citing Zhang and colleagues in *Nature* (2025), he says stable mitochondria send a steady "self" signal to the immune system's dendritic cells; spin chaos from wireless and blue light breaks that signal, so antibodies attack the eye muscles and thyroid.

When heme collapses, histidine, the amino acid that anchors its iron, is diverted into histamine, causing itching and thyroid flares. He ties the rise in thyroid disease since the 1980s to the weakening magnetic field and calls all thyroid disease "altered topology."

He names drug effects too: tetracycline antibiotics blacken the thyroid, and lithium concentrates there and disrupts hormone-making, pushing people toward an underactive thyroid. He calls Hashimoto's thyroiditis pre-cancerous. Eyelid (meibomian) gland trouble is part of the same picture: light and fields thicken the oil to wax and plug the ducts, bulging eyes that don't close in sleep dry the glands out, and the overactive thyroid burns them out.

#### Other eye diseases

The retinal barrier fails along with the body's other barriers, Kruse says. Without a stable field, neuropsin (a UV-A sensor in the eye) can no longer check oxygen's state, the pigment layer's melanin breaks down and macular degeneration speeds up; he reports that a member's father fully reversed his. For glaucoma in the tropics, he says, the driver to remove is artificial light and wireless at night, and a red-light device adds nothing for someone already outdoors all day.

He describes the failing retina as a "frosted lens." Heavy water in the space beneath the retina changes how light bends there, so light scatters off deuterium before melanin can convert it. The light-sensing cells aren't broken; sight fails because the medium does, its dielectric constant (water's capacity to hold charge) falling from a healthy 160 to 78.

Retinitis pigmentosa he calls epigenetic, not genetic. nnEMF and blue light damage the melanin and heme proteins of the retina's pigment layer, the retina's charge falls, and that loss alters the genes blamed for the disease. Phones, Wi-Fi, no grounding and contact lenses worn around the clock, he says, speed it up.

#### In practice

His Graves' and eye protocol: plenty of dawn sun into bare eyes while grounded in a well-pinned field; a bedroom free of wireless; red light or fully blue- and green-blocking glasses after sunset; and nothing that raises metabolic rate, including supplements, peptides and stimulating exercise. For eyelid gland problems he uses sun on the eyes and lids rather than drops. For glaucoma, sun rather than devices, no eyewear outdoors, and total darkness without wireless at night. For retinitis pigmentosa, cut nnEMF, ground or pin, build tolerance to strong sun gradually, and never wear contact lenses around the clock.

- **sources:** [382234](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/382234/), [382239](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/382239/), [382237](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/382237/), [384734](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/384734/), [378299](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/378299/), [375722](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/375722/), [382730](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/382730/), [382384](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/382384/), [377239](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/377239/), [373170](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/373170/), [371637](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/371637/)

### Nerves, head and pain
- **id:** nerve-pain-head-injury
- **pillar:** support
- **app:** hold
- **summary:** Kruse explains CRPS, head and facial trauma, Bell's palsy, voice and hearing loss, tinnitus, facial pain, headaches, spinal-cord softening and osteoporosis as local failures of light, field and fluid.
- **protocols:** nerve-injury, brain-eye-ear-conditions

Kruse explains complex regional pain syndrome, head and facial injuries, Bell's palsy, tinnitus, headaches and osteoporosis as local failures of light, magnetic field and body fluid. In each case he puts grounding and a good field ahead of drugs.

#### Complex regional pain syndrome

CRPS, a severe burning pain that persists after an injury, is in Kruse's model a local crash of melanin and magnetism rather than a mysterious nerve misfire. Melanin, the dark pigment he treats as the tissue's light-to-electricity converter, loses its link to Earth's field in the affected limb. Without "pinning" (being grounded under a stable field), he says light isn't strong enough to settle it, and sunlight or light therapy on an ungrounded limb can speed the damage. So grounding and a good field come first, ahead of nerve blocks or painkillers.

#### Head and facial trauma

Discussing a member hit in the face by a baseball, Kruse read the injury as a "de-pinning" of the sphenoid, the bone at the center of the skull that he treats as the brain's switchboard. The blow, he says, cavitated the spinal fluid and broke up the structured water around the brain's balance centers. Deuterium, a heavy form of hydrogen, then seeped in over the following year, which is why vertigo appeared a year after the injury.

The flattened nose also cut nasal nitric oxide, a gas he says shields incoming oxygen from flipping into a reactive form. His approach is to ground, use a 3% saline method with structural decompression, and get sunlight to restart nasal nitric oxide, rather than relying on anti-vertigo drugs. After a brain bleed with new symptoms, he tells members to get examined and imaged by their surgeon.

#### Bell's palsy, voice and the inner ear

Bell's palsy, a sudden one-sided facial weakness, is today often the first sign that someone is in a "Marcus inversion well," a place whose field stalls the cell's energy chain. Kruse got it himself within a day of arriving in South America; he had standard treatment, but judged the location the cause and cut the trip short.

A voice that gets "stuck" he traces to the far end of the left vagus nerve. In one member he blamed CT scanning, which he says "de-gausses" tissue, plus a compressive "vise" under the arch of the aorta, where the left recurrent laryngeal nerve (the branch that runs the voice box) loops back up. The nerve then acts like a heat-losing resistor instead of a clean conductor, and he compares it to astronauts losing function in the brain's speech area once they leave Earth's field.

Tinnitus, he says, is the inner ear's fluid losing its structure, so the hair cells generate noise. Astronauts develop it within days in orbit, and on Earth it means something in the environment must be removed. For Ménière's disease he warns that a diuretic makes it worse. Hearing loss carried through bone, he adds, can be repaired with light, but where someone lives sets a ceiling: a place with a decent field but poor UV and near-infrared light limits how far repair can go.

#### Headaches and facial pain

Headache, brain swelling and seizures come in his model from spinal fluid losing its charge. The fluid thickens, drains poorly and pressure rises, and solar storms can tip a vulnerable brain into this state. Migraines he attributes to deuterium loading in the trigeminal (Gasserian) ganglion, a nerve hub beside the brain.

Right-sided facial pain follows the drainage. The right lymphatic duct and the right-side veins carry most of the brain's waste fluid, Kruse says; when that fluid is deuterium-heavy and slow, it presses on the first branch of the trigeminal nerve in Meckel's cave, a fluid pocket beside the brainstem, causing a pressure-type trigeminal neuralgia felt in the right eye.

#### The spinal cord

Meckel's cave matters for the spinal cord too. He says it supplies the mechanical "dither" that keeps water in the fourth ventricle moving, so carbon dioxide from the blood is stirred in, deuterium can be separated out, and the vagal region can clear it from a damaged stretch of cord. That is why he holds that myelomalacia, a softening of the spinal cord usually seen as permanent, can reverse, and why he looks for a blockage at Meckel's cave, often alongside sleep problems.

#### Bone and osteoporosis

Bone is not a calcium rock, Kruse says, but a crystal-and-collagen semiconductor held together at its junctions by copper-65. Under Earth's field, walking and other loading generate a steady current that charges bone and tells bone-building cells to keep working. In space, or under nnEMF (wireless and wiring fields) and night light on Earth, the copper loses its alignment and is pushed out, the bone's charge flips and osteoporosis follows. With the field weakening, he says, Earth now behaves increasingly like space.

Citing Becker's work on bone growing in response to magnetic pressure, he reads a bone spur (osteophyte) on the spine as a calcified shadow of magnetic turbulence, laid down at the level where deuterium stalled, and calls such spurs "magnetic tafoni," after the honeycomb hollows wind carves in rock.

#### In practice

His nerve-injury protocol: for CRPS, secure grounding and a good field before putting sun or light on the limb; for head or face trauma with vertigo, ground, use his 3% saline approach with structural decompression, and get sunlight for nasal nitric oxide; after a brain bleed with new symptoms, get examined and imaged; and if Bell's palsy appears soon after arriving somewhere, suspect the place. For Ménière's, avoid diuretics. For hearing loss and spinal-cord softening he relies on light and on clearing the fluid routes rather than writing them off. He cautions against making nerve blocks, painkillers or anti-vertigo drugs the main strategy.

- **sources:** [381930](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/381930/), [381468](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/381468/), [381953](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/381953/), [385704](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/385704/), [384185](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/384185/), [377363](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/377363/), [385877](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/385877/), [376041](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/376041/), [382943](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/382943/), [373261](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/373261/), [373274](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/373274/), [370740](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/370740/)

### Pregnancy and birth
- **id:** pregnancy-and-birth
- **pillar:** support
- **app:** hold
- **summary:** Kruse says a pregnant woman needs grounded sun more than extra calories, treats labor and nausea as functional signals, and links parents' deuterium load to birth defects and to the child's later insulin resistance.
- **protocols:** pregnancy-sun, pregnancy-birth-children

Kruse says a pregnant woman needs grounded sunlight more than extra calories. He reads severe pregnancy nausea and labor pain as purposeful signals rather than problems to suppress, and links the parents' load of deuterium, a heavy form of hydrogen, at conception to birth defects.

#### Sun before calories

Standard prenatal advice, in Kruse's view, sets women up for gestational diabetes and preeclampsia: avoid the sun, wear chemical sunscreen, eat a calorie surplus and take synthetic prenatal pills. In a sun-starved mother surrounded by wireless signals, he says, extra food only piles up unused electrons and loads deuterium into the liver.

Grounded, full-spectrum sun does the opposite in his account. Near-infrared light hydrates the mother's melanin (the light-absorbing pigment), UV light anchors her organ clocks through light sensors in the skin and eyes, and her blood carries the sun's energy to the placenta, so she doesn't need to overeat.

The placenta, he says, is the fetus's stand-in ground, which he calls a "placental stator." Because the fetus can't touch the Earth, the placenta's twisted, one-handed villi act as a giant spin filter, creating a small magnetic field under the fetus that keeps its energy chain working during rapid growth.

The mother is also the baby's filter, or "fab plant." Nature, in his account, meant her to turn plant vitamin K1 into MK-4, the animal form of vitamin K, filtering out deuterium on the way, and to pass it on slowly through breast milk. The placenta likewise meters vitamin K on purpose, so babies are not "born deficient."

#### Nausea, the heart and early warnings

Severe pregnancy nausea (hyperemesis gravidarum) is a protective brain alarm in Kruse's model. When deuterium floods the mother's blood, a stress hormone called GDF15 acts on the vomiting center at the floor of the brain. It stops her eating so she burns fat, which makes the clean, low-deuterium water the embryo needs to copy its mitochondria cleanly. Anti-nausea drugs silence the alarm without fixing its cause.

Pregnancy is a huge draw on the mother's mitochondria, he says. If her body water is loaded with deuterium, her heart muscle can tear itself apart trying to spin its energy motors "against concrete," as happens to astronauts. He sees Takotsubo or "broken-heart" cardiomyopathy, a sudden ballooning of the heart, as more than a grief reaction: anything that suddenly destroys melanin and the hormones made along its pathway (dopamine, noradrenaline, adrenaline) can dilate the heart, and nnEMF and a weakening field both qualify.

If the stress continues, he says, the fetus's liver raises alpha-fetoprotein (AFP), the protein measured in maternal blood tests, as a distress flare and shield. Morning sickness and newborn jaundice are, to him, early warnings of an oxygen problem before birth that later shows up as eye and skin disease.

#### Labor as an ignition switch

Labor pain is a "spintronic ignition switch," Kruse says. Contractions squeeze tissue the way pressure squeezes a crystal, sending an electrical surge up the spine that switches on oxytocin, endorphins and anti-inflammatory signals. Epidurals cut that signal, and he links them to worse tearing, breastfeeding trouble and postpartum fog. The squeeze of the birth canal and the mother's vaginal microbes, he adds, set the newborn's immune baseline.

#### Conception and after

Parents who conceive under light stress, wireless exposure and no grounding, Kruse says, can't make low-deuterium water, so their sperm and eggs fill with deuterium. The embryo's spine needs very fluid water to lengthen; under the heavy load it protects itself with scar-like collagen that tethers the spinal cord to the base of the spine. He links the same germline load to heart defects.

The load carries into later life. Skin oils and the fat under the skin should act as deuterium sinks, but a mother with heavy body water, processed carbohydrates, little sun and a weak field forces the fetus to build its machinery with heavy hydrogen. Cells that can't make energy then cut back their insulin receptors to avoid damage, which he calls the true origin of insulin resistance, type 2 diabetes and obesity. For the child, he adds, a healthy mother of modest intellect is a better start than a brilliant one who is heavily deuterated, eats from a deuterium-heavy region and waits until 40 to conceive.

Postpartum depression, like sleep apnea, is in his view a disease of lost magnetism. The mother must become magnetically "pinned" (grounded under a stable, strong field) to let old depressive memories go and start fresh, and he says moving to Florida is not enough.

#### In practice

His pregnancy protocols: plenty of grounded sun with UV and infrared, including bare-eyed morning light; no chemical sunscreen or sun avoidance; no forced calorie surplus and no synthetic prenatal pills; and a wireless-free home, fixing the environment first in a high-risk pregnancy. He wants both parents' deuterium load dealt with before conception. He favors unmedicated vaginal birth without an epidural where possible, and breastfeeding. For severe nausea he says to fix light, grounding and field rather than taking anti-nausea drugs, and for postpartum depression, light, water, magnetism and pinning rather than SSRIs.

- **sources:** [384323](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/384323/), [382424](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/382424/), [382425](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/382425/), [380942](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/380942/), [380967](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/380967/), [380128](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/380128/), [380129](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/380129/), [372298](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/372298/), [373363](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/373363/), [372980](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/372980/)

### Children, infants and autism
- **id:** children-and-autism
- **pillar:** support
- **app:** hold
- **summary:** Kruse calls autism a "magnetic decline disease" and warns that indoor childhood, blue-light phototherapy, the newborn vitamin K shot and early deuterium load set children up for lifelong problems.
- **protocols:** pregnancy-birth-children

Kruse calls autism a "magnetic decline disease" and says an indoor childhood, blue-light treatment for newborn jaundice and an early overload of deuterium (a heavy form of hydrogen) set children up for lifelong problems. Children have less buffering than adults, in his view, so their environment matters even more.

#### Autism as magnetic decline

Building on Douglas Wallace's work linking certain mitochondrial haplogroups (maternal lineages) to higher odds of autism, Kruse says a vulnerable lineage living in a mismatched place crosses an energy threshold. The brain uses about 20% of the body's energy at 2% of its mass, so it is the first to fail. A high-declination city, where compass north strays far from true north, combined with heavy nnEMF (wireless and wiring fields), pushes the brain's energy chain into a stall.

He adds that the fusion site on chromosome 2 trips under field loss and nnEMF, that autism rates track declination, and that a mother's vitamin D deficiency adds risk. He predicts autism should be rarest where agonic lines (zero-declination lines) cross the tropics or the equator.

Autistic sensory reactions, Kruse says, are protective triage: a brain that can't process everything sheds costly input, and a vacuum cleaner's noise sets off an alarm in unbuffered tissue. Autistic children sleep badly because a weak planetary field, LED light and indoor EMF disrupt their body clock.

#### Infants and the unfinished vagus

An infant's vagus nerve is not yet myelinated (insulated), so in Kruse's model it can't cope with a deuterium overload. Deuterium jams the baby's energy motors, and the 2 Hz rhythm he believes keeps tissues in sync breaks down. The baby becomes a "foreign astronaut" in its own crib, unable to lock its clocks to the sun and planet, and is set up for developmental delay, sensory problems, gut imbalance, broken sleep and early metabolic trouble. How badly a child is hit, he says, depends on the mother's condition before and during pregnancy.

Blue-light phototherapy for newborn jaundice, which he says replaced full-spectrum light over the past 35 years, permanently disrupts the clock run by heme, the iron-containing molecule in blood. Citing Oláh and colleagues (2013), he notes more atypical moles, a melanoma risk marker, in children who had it.

He groups cradle cap (infant seborrheic dermatitis) with jaundice as an early sign of mitochondrial trouble rather than a passing effect of the mother's hormones, since children who have it show more metabolic problems later. Skin, to him, is the outward map of how efficiently mitochondria run, and in adults the same rash is a recognized marker of metabolic syndrome.

#### The newborn vitamin K shot

Kruse questions the routine vitamin K injection at birth. Turning the injected K1 into MK-4, the form mammals use to manage calcium, has to pass through menadione, a step that uses up glutathione, which he calls the cell's main defense against deuterium and oxidative stress; he likens the drain to what acetaminophen does in adults. Nature delivers vitamin K as a "slow leak" through the placenta and breast milk, and a large dose is, to him, a high-voltage event in a low-voltage infant whose detox systems aren't yet running.

In a hospital full of nnEMF he calls the shot an "nnEMF multiplier." With glutathione drained, the inner mitochondrial membrane fails, ATP synthase slows on deuterium-laden fuel, and the brain fails to prune its connections and lay down myelin. He calls this "isotopic ossification" and links it to developmental delay and autism.

#### Indoor childhood

Children kept indoors under blue light and away from the ground are, to Kruse, low-capacitance "astronauts" with no outside field to guide their electrons. PANS/PANDAS, the sudden onset of tics, rage and OCD often blamed on strep, he calls a disorder of a deuterated terrain, set up by desert living, wireless-heavy classrooms, blue light and deuterium-rich tap water. Strep, in his account, arrives as a clean-up crew, so antibiotics kill the helpers.

Pinworms, likewise, settle in the still, heavy-water "ponds" of a child whose drainage routes aren't flushing, and chronic infections in children mean both child and parents are carrying deuterium. The worms' night itching, he adds, blocks the brain's deep-sleep clean-up.

He says GLP-1 weight-loss drugs leave about 1 in 8 children with vitamin D deficiency within a year. Bedwetting is often a deuterium-loaded skull base acting on the pituitary's water-balance signal, not a behavior problem or a missing hormone.

#### In practice

His pregnancy, birth and childhood protocol: no blue-light phototherapy for newborn jaundice; no heavy deuterium load for an infant before its nerves are insulated, which for him includes a skeptical look at the high-dose vitamin K shot; and children kept grounded. For autistic children who sleep badly, cut night-time LED light and indoor EMF. For PANS/PANDAS, take the child out of wireless-heavy rooms, put bare feet on the earth, use full sun including near-infrared, and favor deuterium depletion over repeated antibiotics. No GLP-1 drugs for children. For autism generally he suggests living on or near an agonic line, ideally in the tropics.

- **sources:** [383989](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/383989/), [383988](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/383988/), [384985](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/384985/), [383901](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/383901/), [379276](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/379276/), [380942](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/380942/), [384272](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/384272/), [385644](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/385644/), [378082](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/378082/), [373362](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/373362/), [372980](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/372980/), [370870](https://forum.jackkruse.com/posts/370870/)

### Fertility, cycles and pelvic voltage
- **id:** fertility-cycles-voltage
- **pillar:** support
- **app:** hold
- **summary:** Kruse ties menstrual rhythm, egg and sperm quality, erections and orgasm to lunar ground currents, sunlight and pelvic voltage, which devices and indoor life collapse.
- **protocols:** moon-cycle-routine, male-fertility

Kruse ties menstrual rhythm, erections and sperm quality to sunlight, moon-driven currents in the ground and the electrical charge of pelvic tissue. Devices, high-rise living and indoor life collapse that charge, he says, and fertility shows it first because one pathway governs both fertility and longevity.

#### Sperm as the purest test

Sperm gave up their repair machinery to travel light, Kruse says, so they can't compensate when the environment fails, which makes them the clearest readout of where a man lives. If DNA damage in sperm climbs from about 20% to 35% within a few months, he reads that as a new, ongoing stressor acting across two or three of the 74–90-day sperm production cycles.

In his example the stressor was a move to a 24th-floor apartment. High up, he says, the body sits thousands of volts above the ground, unable to draw electrons from the Earth, and acts like an ungrounded antenna. Deuterium, a heavy form of hydrogen, then adds mass to the sperm's DNA so that its copying machinery stalls and breaks.

#### Eggs kept offline

Eggs take the opposite strategy. Citing a *Nature* paper showing that egg cells suppress Complex I, the energy chain's entry point and its main source of reactive oxygen, Kruse says the female germline is kept largely offline from before birth, like a satellite powering down to cross a radiation zone. That lets an egg stay a quiet "quantum seed" for decades.

Egg quality is falling, he says, because night light, nnEMF and the weakening field force Complex I back on, so the egg burns deuterium-heavy fuel and "browns" from within. Citing a 2026 paper in which long days (18 hours of light, 6 of dark) disrupted the ovary's NAD+ metabolism (a key electron carrier) and impaired its follicles, he calls a long light day a trap that mimics indoor "magnetic darkness."

A broken body clock also stalls the vagus-driven bicarbonate flush that clears deuterium from the gut, sending it into the pelvis and the follicular fluid around the egg. The follicle then shifts to fermentation and fails to ovulate, and he expects ovarian failure to cluster where the field is weakest.

#### Cycles and the moon

Citing Harold Saxton Burr's recordings of tree voltage and the work of Georges Lakhovsky, Kruse says living tissue tracks the moon, the sun and magnetic storms. Moon-driven ground currents (telluric currents) once synchronized women's cycles to a place's "electromagnetic terroir," the way they time coral spawning a few days after the late-spring full moon. Women living together in nature, he says, synchronize like pendulum clocks on a shared wall, and city light and a weakened field break both rhythms.

#### Hormones as stand-ins for charge

Hormones, in Kruse's model, are stand-ins for the body's local electrical charge. Night light and nnEMF (wireless and wiring fields) damage melanin and the eye's daylight sensor, and lack of grounding drains charge from the mitochondria.

Sex hormones start as cholesterol turned into pregnenolone inside the mitochondria by iron-containing enzymes that depend on a stable field. In a weak field, he says, the cell diverts pregnenolone to the stress hormone cortisol to survive, which he calls the "pregnenolone steal." Progesterone then crashes and the monthly cycle fades; he warns of widespread infertility, endometriosis and early menopause. Heavy menstrual bleeding signals a missing "Zeeman handshake," his term for the field keeping electron spins aligned.

#### Erectile dysfunction and orgasm

Kruse calls erectile dysfunction the male version of a lost cycle: a whole-body voltage collapse, not a plumbing problem. An erection needs nitric oxide from an iron-containing enzyme that depends on precise electron spins. A phone in the pocket or a laptop on the lap exposes the groin to alternating fields that, citing Liboff and Blackman's work on ion cyclotron resonance, pull calcium out of cells and drop pelvic voltage, while the pregnenolone steal sends cholesterol to cortisol instead of testosterone. Ancestral men, he says, stayed virile because full sun and bare feet kept their pelvic tissue charged.

Orgasm, in his model, is a fluid surge that pushes the brain into an urgent "de-frag," which is why sleep afterward is often the deepest dream sleep. Female ejaculation, fluid from the Skene's glands around the urethra (the female counterpart of the prostate), is to him a normal, healthy flush of the pelvic nerve network, not incontinence. Inability to climax, dementia and thyroid disease share one culprit in his view: a clogged deuterium exhaust.

He sees the lower spine and pelvis as a sump below the brainstem where heavy water settles, and female ejaculation as its "lumbo-sacral de-frag": strong pelvic-floor contractions spin deuterium out of the Skene's glands. Most women, he notes, don't know these glands make PSA (prostate-specific antigen). PSA is a protease, an enzyme that cuts proteins, and he suggests it acts as a local cleaner for the protein "sludge" that ubiquitin has tagged for removal once deuterium disturbs it, so the fluid is the isotopic and protein exhaust of a cellular reboot. He links this periodic flush to women's lower rate of ALS. A light-starved perineum, in his account, lets pelvic melanin slide back into its precursors and weakens the flush.

Climax gets harder, he says, because dopamine can't drive the pelvic muscles against a thicker, deuterium-heavy base. Intravenous 3% saline, given by a clinician, pulls that heavy fluid out of the pelvic tissue into the blood to be cleared, lowering viscosity so dopamine can work again; he suggests timing it three to five days before a period so the body sheds the load with it.

#### In practice

For men his protocol is to take phones and laptops out of pockets and off the lap and clear alternating-current sources from the workspace; use groin cold (ice packs or cold plunges); and sleep on a grounded sheet with the bed aligned north–south. For sperm DNA damage, live at ground level (or shield and ground the bed) and drink certified deuterium-depleted water under 105 ppm for 90 days, one full production cycle. Before conceiving, he wants both partners' deuterium load addressed. For women with difficult climax he points to UV-A sunlight on the perineum and, with a clinician, the pre-period 3% saline flush. His moon-cycle routine, written to restore the menstrual cycle, calls for strict darkness or full blue-blocking after sunset (strictest around the full moon), morning sun, 20–60 minutes of barefoot grounding a day with more near full and new moons, time in stronger-field places, cool dark sleep and cold, and tracking cycle, mood and heart-rate variability over two to three lunar months.

### GLP-1 drugs, anorexia and the lost fat capacitor
- **id:** glp1-drug-damage
- **pillar:** support
- **app:** hold
- **summary:** Kruse says GLP-1 weight-loss drugs force a crude deuterium flush that ends in lost pleasure, lost muscle and collagen, a hijacked vagus, vision loss and a "welded-shut" exhaust, and that anorexia shares the damage without the fat buffer.
- **protocols:** glp1-damage-offset, anorexia-recovery

Kruse considers GLP-1 weight-loss drugs (semaglutide, tirzepatide, retatrutide) harmful overall. In his model they force a crude, off-schedule flush of deuterium, a heavy form of hydrogen. The flush brings short-term benefits but leaves people unable to feel pleasure, losing muscle and collagen, and with the body's own deuterium exhaust shut. He groups anorexia with them because it shares the damage but lacks the body fat that would buffer it.

#### The exhaust the drugs hijack

Kruse's starting point is the body's natural deuterium exit. Morning sunlight's infrared triggers the cortisol awakening response, the hormone rise after waking. That switches on the glucagon gene on chromosome 2, the same gene that makes GLP-1 in the gut, and releases about 2 liters of bicarbonate fluid from the pancreas. Guided by the vagus nerve, the flush carries deuterium out through the gut.

Much modern hunger, he says, is a false famine. Deuterium-heavy food eaten indoors under LEDs jams ATP synthase, the cell's energy motor, and the brain reads the resulting electron shortage as a lack of calories; he calls this the "hunger mirror." GLP-1 drugs silence that signal without fixing what sends it.

#### A crude flush

The injected drug, Kruse says, bypasses sunlight's timing and forces a continuous, crude deuterium purge, a "quantum centrifuge." For a while this clears deuterium from blood-vessel linings and kidney filters. That, he argues, explains the roughly 20% drop in heart and kidney events in the SELECT and FLOW trials, which did not track weight loss. He calls it a "cardioprotective illusion": a real effect, but not a cure, and paid for later in the ways below.

#### Loss of pleasure

The first cost is anhedonia, a loss of pleasure that he distinguishes from depression because there is no guilt or worthlessness. The drugs act in the brainstem and reward centers and damp the pulsed dopamine surges behind wanting. He adds that the forced clamp on mitochondrial energy leaves none of the loose fluctuation dopamine needs. Users lose interest not only in food but in sex, work, hobbies and friends, and he wants this screened with the SHAPS pleasure scale rather than depression questionnaires.

#### Muscle, collagen and hair

Citing Helen Blau's lab at Stanford, Kruse says semaglutide raises 15-PGDH, an aging enzyme he calls a "gerozyme." It breaks down prostaglandin E2, a repair signal, and so locks muscle stem cells in dormancy. With muscle unable to repair, even moderate exercise acts like overtraining.

Any drug that drives up metabolic rate, he says, forces ATP synthase to turn against a deuterium-heavy, "concrete" medium and damages the junctions of the inner mitochondrial membrane. GLP-1 drugs do the same, which he compares to the muscle astronauts lose in space, and he sees an underactive thyroid as the body's adaptive slowdown under deuterium.

In his model the drugs plus nnEMF (wireless and wiring fields) stall the energy chain and flip oxygen into its reactive singlet form. Mitochondrial DNA then leaks out, sets off the cell's virus alarms and jams the 2 Hz rhythm he says stem cells listen to. The stem-cell niche is left overheated and dried out, a state he calls "focal lattice lock."

The multi-agonists that also hit the glucagon receptor (tirzepatide and retatrutide) push tissue further, he says. The chromosome 2 "fuse" blows, and muscle and collagen revert to loose, primitive tissue, so he expects hypermobile, Ehlers-Danlos-like connective tissue and 35-year-olds with the bones of 80-year-olds. In type 2 diabetics, whose energy chain is already stalled, the calorie cut comes out of muscle rather than fat.

Hair loss is triage. A sudden shock and a reduced supply of nutrients push many follicles into their shedding phase about two to four months in, and losing weight faster than 3–4 lb a week is the strongest predictor. He watches ferritin, zinc, vitamin D3, B12 and protein, notes that about 1 in 8 children on these drugs develop vitamin D deficiency within a year, and estimates the drugs cost ten to twenty years of life.

#### The welded-shut exhaust

While the drug forces its flush, Kruse says, the natural route idles and fails. GLP-1 drugs cut the pancreas's bicarbonate output and slow the gut, and GLP-1 receptors on the vagus nerve's sensory hub (the nodose ganglion) overload with deuterium; gastroparesis, bowel problems and fatty liver follow. The liver's P450 enzymes, which process steroids and fat-soluble vitamins, are scrambled. In his view users move from a stalled small intestine to Crohn's disease and then gut cancer, and the deuterium backing up from the gut can reach the brain and set up Parkinson's.

He points to 3,636 active lawsuits gathered in one federal case (MDL 3094), mostly over gastroparesis, a paralyzed stomach, as evidence that the drugs hijack the vagus. Without the nerve's electrical instructions the stomach lining stalls, building up deuterium and low oxygen. Low oxygen also blocks DHA, the omega-3 fat that needs high oxygen pressure to enter the brain, cutting the brain off from light-driven melanin repair.

The same lawsuits now include permanent vision loss from NAION, a stroke of the optic nerve, which he calls the eye syndrome of spaceflight produced on Earth. The optic nerve is so dense with mitochondria that deuterium easily locks it up, and its UV-A light sensor can no longer turn light into electricity.

He compares the drugs to Pervitin, the methamphetamine given to soldiers in the Second World War: both flood the body with CLIP, a misprocessed fragment of the hormone precursor POMC, and "weld shut" the vagal bicarbonate exhaust. When the drug stops, deuterium backs into the insulin-making beta cells, POMC keeps yielding CLIP instead of fat-burning alpha-MSH, and hunger rebounds frantically, with weight returning as fat alongside inflammation and insulin resistance. Grey-market retatrutide is worse still, he says, and he predicts a rise in insulin-secreting tumors (insulinomas).

#### Anorexia: the same damage without the buffer

Anorexia, in Kruse's account, shares the GLP-1 damage but lacks the "fat capacitor," body fat acting as an electrical buffer and energy store. Leptin, the fat hormone he calls the master photoelectric transducer because it reports stored energy to the brain, approaches zero. Its "carrier wave" vanishes and the brain reads endless starvation, producing distorted body image and obsessive loops. The starved gut has also wasted, so it can't extract enough energy from food.

#### In practice

Kruse's GLP-1 damage protocol has four core steps: morning sun where the field is clean, ideally at zero declination and away from all wireless; deuterium-depleted water at 90–105 ppm with frequent cold immersion at 45–55 °F; unshielded early-morning infrared and UV-A on skin and eyes; and pure, fresh (2S)-hesperidin timed to sun exposure. For gut damage he adds the "bicarb hack," oral baking soda to restart the flush, and he rules the drugs out for children. His anorexia recovery base keeps the same foundation but replaces ice baths with short, gentle dips in 80–85 °F water, going colder only as fat returns. It maximizes morning UV-A and infrared so melanin can supply charge directly, bypassing the weakened gut, and adds liquid ionic magnesium and trace copper-histidine timed with the morning sun.
